--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: What are the standard exercise dates for the Guaranteed Insurability Option in life insurance policies?
reasoning: The query asks about "Guaranteed Insurability Option exercise dates," which refers to specific dates when a policyholder can exercise their right to increase life insurance coverage without additional underwriting, typically under a life insurance policy. This is a well-defined feature in certain insurance products, particularly permanent life insurance policies.

The key terms here are "Guaranteed Insurability Option" and "exercise dates." The term "exercise dates" implies specific, predefined times (e.g., anniversaries of the policy, specific ages) when the option can be used. Since this is a policy-specific feature, the answer depends on the policy terms, but the query is asking for the general rule or standard dates associated with this option.

This is a single-fact query: it seeks a specific piece of information (the dates) tied to a known insurance feature. There is no need to chain through multiple facts—no intermediate entity needs to be identified first. The answer can be directly retrieved from policy documentation or insurance industry standards.

Therefore, only one sub-goal is needed: to identify the standard exercise dates for the Guaranteed Insurability Option.
